**Architecture.** A **DQN or PPO agent** whose state is a vector of channel statistics (erasure rate, burst length distribution, per-position reliability from M1/M5) and whose action is a discrete choice from a bank of degree distributions (or a parameterised distribution family). Reward is the **completion time** or **overhead ratio** for the current file, measured online. Pre-train in simulation with the synthetic channel generator (M13), then fine-tune on-device with **conservative policy updates** so that a bad exploration does not stall a live transfer.

**Alternative for low-power devices.** If RL is too expensive on-device, train a small **supervised model** offline: for each channel state, compute the optimal distribution via offline search, and distill the mapping into a lookup table or tiny MLP. This is the practical production path for older phones.

**Deployment.** Cloud or on-device inference via a small MLP. The RL training happens offline; the on-device model is a policy network or a distilled table.

---

## 5. Kinetic-specific models (M8–M10): tracking, deblurring, and synchronisation

### M8. Face Tracker and Rotation Estimator

**Gap.** The rotating mass is a non-rigid, self-occluding object. Classical feature tracking fails when faces are partially visible and lighting changes. The decoder needs to know **which face is visible, what its rotation phase is, and how much motion blur to expect** before it can even attempt tile localisation.

**Architecture.** A **temporal CNN + recurrent head** (e.g., a lightweight 3D CNN or a CNN backbone with a GRU) that takes a short sequence of frames and outputs:

- A segmentation mask of the mass.
- A probability distribution over face identities (from the face header).
- A rotation phase estimate \(\hat{\theta}\) and angular velocity \(\hat{\omega}\).
- A per-face blur kernel estimate.

Train with synthetic sequences from a **differentiable rotation renderer** (part of M13) and fine-tune on real captures. The face-identity head can be trained with a classification loss using the known face headers; the phase head with a sinusoidal regression loss.

**Deployment.** GPU / NNAPI. Run at the camera frame rate. The tracker provides the **crop window and phase** for M2, so M2 does not need to search the whole frame.

### M9. Motion Deblurrer

**Gap.** At 0.25–0.5 Hz rotation, motion blur is modest but not negligible, and rolling-shutter distortion can mix adjacent faces. A single-frame deblurrer trained on generic motion blur will not remove **rotation-specific** blur.

**Architecture.** A **deformable convolutional network** or a **video transformer** that takes a short burst of frames and the estimated rotation phase/velocity from M8, and outputs a deblurred, rectified face. Condition the deblurrer on \(\hat{\omega}\): the blur kernel is a function of angular velocity, so the network should be explicitly told the velocity. Use a **kernel-free** design (direct image-to-image) with a perceptual loss and a **decode-in-the-loop loss** (same principle as M3).

**Training.** Synthetic blur generated by the rotation renderer with realistic shutter speeds and rolling-shutter simulation. Fine-tune on real paired data where a high-speed camera captures the same face as the target low-speed camera.

**Deployment.** GPU. Run only on faces that M8 marks as “blurred but potentially decodable”. The rest of the frames bypass M9.

### M10. Motor Synchroniser

**Gap.** The face-rate matching rule (\(F/T \le R_{\text{camera}}\)) assumes the motor speed is known and stable. In reality, belt slip, load variation, and motor nonlinearity cause drift. The system must **adapt rotation speed to the receiver’s actual fps** without a back-channel.

**Architecture.** A **reinforcement learning agent (SAC or DDPG)** that controls motor voltage/current. State: estimated camera fps (from inter-frame timing), estimated blur level (from M8), and estimated decode success rate (from M1/M6). Action: target angular velocity. Reward: decode throughput minus a blur penalty. A review of RL for motor control notes that RL handles nonlinearities and parameter uncertainties better than classical control. Motion Hologram demonstrates joint optimisation of display content and motion planning via RL, which is conceptually adjacent.

**Safer alternative.** For a production system, a **model-predictive controller with learned dynamics** may be preferable to a pure RL agent. Train a small dynamics model (angular velocity → blur, fps → capture success) and solve a short-horizon optimisation at each control step. This is more interpretable and safer for a physical motor.

**Deployment.** MCU or BLE bridge, not the phone. The phone sends the target fps and observed decode rate over BLE; the motor controller runs the policy locally.

---

## 6. Shared state and adaptation (M11): the transformer that binds the stack

**Gap.** Each model above operates on its own input. But the system needs a **global channel state** so that M3, M6, M7, and M10 can make coordinated decisions. This state includes: blur level, colour shift magnitude, perspective angle, SNR, erasure rate, burst length, and recent decode success.

**Architecture.** A small **transformer or GRU** that takes a sequence of per-frame feature vectors (from M1, M4, M8) and outputs a **channel state embedding**. This embedding is fed to:

- M3 to select the SR strength.
- M6 to set the decoder’s damping/offset parameters.
- M7 to select the fountain degree distribution.
- M10 to adjust motor speed.
- M1 to calibrate its decodability threshold.

The transformer’s self-attention is well suited to modelling the **temporal correlation** of screen-camera channels (e.g., a sudden glare affects several consecutive frames). Work on transformers for optical communication explicitly identifies equalisation, modulation classification, and channel estimation as tasks where self-attention can model complex interactions.

**Training.** Self-supervised: predict the next frame’s channel features from the current sequence. Fine-tune with a **throughput reward** (end-to-end) using policy gradient, so the embedding is optimised for the metrics that matter.

**Deployment.** CPU. The model is tiny (a few layers, 64–128 dimensions) and runs once per frame.

---

## 7. Production and training infrastructure (M12–M13)

### M12. Print Defect Inspector

**Gap.** A printed mosaic with a single defective tile can reduce throughput or, if the defect is systematic, make a page undecodable. Industrial QR defect classification has reached **99.00% accuracy** with a lightweight vision transformer with embedded hybrid attention (ViT-EHA), and lightweight label defect systems report **Top-1 accuracy of 99.25%** on custom datasets.

**Architecture.** A lightweight ViT (e.g., MobileViT or a distilled DeiT) with hybrid attention, trained on **defect classes** relevant to this system: ink bleed, colour misregistration, missing tile, smudge, fold crease, specular hotspot. The model runs on a camera image of the printed page **before distribution**, flagging pages that need reprinting.

**Training data.** Real printed samples with controlled defects, plus synthetic defects applied to clean renders. Use **few-shot learning** for rare defect types.

**Deployment.** Cloud or edge GPU at the print production stage, not on the scanning phone. This is a quality-control model, not a runtime decoder model.

### M13. Synthetic Channel Generator (the training engine)

**Every model above depends on data that does not exist yet.** You cannot collect millions of real screen-camera captures across every lighting condition, print defect, rotation speed, and camera model. The **synthetic channel generator is the most important model in the project** because it produces the training distribution for all the others.

**Architecture.** A hybrid of:

- **Differentiable renderer**: physics-based simulation of print (ink spread, dot gain), display (gamma, subpixel geometry), camera (lens PSF, sensor noise, demosaicing, JPEG), and rotation (motion blur, rolling shutter).
- **GAN-based domain adaptation**: a GAN that takes synthetic renders and makes them statistically indistinguishable from real captures. GANOCCAS demonstrates a generative adversarial framework for integrated optical camera communication and scene sensing that reduces interference and reconstructs images across SNR scenarios. Stripe image processing patents use GANs with U-Net generators and SimAM attention for camera optical communication.
- **Adversarial hard-example generator**: a generator that produces **channel realisations** that maximise the decoder’s error rate. This is adversarial training for the whole stack: the generator learns to create blur/colour/occlusion patterns that break M5 and M6, and the decoders learn to survive them.

**Training loop.** Alternate between:

1. Training the channel generator to maximise end-to-end symbol error rate.
2. Training the decoders (M1–M6) to minimise end-to-end symbol error rate and frame error rate.

This is a **minimax game** that produces a decoder robust to the worst channel conditions the generator can invent—a far stronger guarantee than training on a fixed dataset.

**Sim-to-real transfer.** After adversarial training in simulation, fine-tune on a small set of real captures using **domain randomisation** (vary camera model, lighting, print material) and **test-time adaptation** (M4 already adapts per frame). This closes the reality gap without requiring millions of real labels.

---

## 8. Training strategy: multi-task, multi-stage, and self-supervised

### Stage 1: Synthetic pre-training (weeks, cloud)

Train M2, M3, M5, M8, and M9 on synthetic data from M13. Use large batches and standard augmentations. The goal is **broad coverage**, not perfection.

### Stage 2: Adversarial co-training (weeks, cloud)

Train M1–M7 in the minimax loop with M13. This is where the models learn the **tail of the channel distribution**: glare, folds, extreme perspective, motor vibration.

### Stage 3: Real-data fine-tuning (days, on-device or cloud)

Collect a modest real dataset (10k–100k frames) across a diverse set of phones, including low-end devices. Fine-tune M4, M5, and M6 with **knowledge distillation** from the cloud models to the zero-overhead deployment format. M1 and M11 can be fine-tuned with **federated learning** across user devices without uploading raw frames.

### Stage 4: Continual on-device adaptation

M4 and M11 adapt **unsupervised** every session: M4 from pilots, M11 from temporal prediction. M1 can adapt its threshold from the observed decode success rate. This keeps the models calibrated to the user’s specific phone and environment without retraining.

---

## 9. Deployment on Android: zero-overhead and NNAPI

The production constraint is brutal: the decoder must run on phones from 2020 with 4 GB RAM and no dedicated NPU. The deployment strategy is therefore **tiered**:

| Tier | Models | Runtime | Latency target |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Zero-overhead (CPU, Rust/C++)** | M5 (shape–colour), M6 (boosted NMS), M4 (colour MLP) | Hand-written matrix ops, no DL framework | <10 ms per tile |
| **NNAPI / GPU delegate** | M1, M2, M8, M11 | LiteRT with NNAPI or GPU delegate | 5–20 ms per frame |
| **On-demand GPU** | M3, M9 | LiteRT GPU delegate, only when triggered | 50–200 ms per tile |
| **Cloud / offline** | M7 (training), M12, M13 | Not on device | N/A |

**Zero-overhead inference** is the key enabler for older hardware. 4SQR-CodeScanner shows that loading weights and biases directly and performing optimised matrix operations eliminates the deep learning framework runtime, reducing peak memory to ~6 MB and decoding time to ~65 ms. Implement M5 and M6 in Rust with `jni-rs`, export weights as flat `f32` or `i8` arrays, and use SIMD intrinsics for the matrix multiplies.

**Quantisation.** M1, M2, and M8 can be quantised to INT8 with minimal accuracy loss. M5 should be quantised to INT8 only after calibration; use **quantisation-aware training** to preserve the soft-probability calibration that M6 depends on. M3 and M9 remain FP16 on GPU.

**NNAPI and LiteRT.** Android 10+ provides the Neural Networks API (NNAPI) for hardware acceleration, and LiteRT (TensorFlow Lite runtime) executes application-owned models with platform acceleration and CPU fallback. Use NNAPI for M2 and M8; use the GPU delegate for M3 and M9. For M1 and M11, CPU is often sufficient and avoids the NNAPI initialisation overhead on older devices.

**Model size budget.** Total on-device model footprint should stay under **30 MB**:

- M1: 1–2 MB (INT8).
- M2: 5–8 MB (INT8 YOLOv8n-class).
- M4: <100 KB.
- M5: 1–2 MB (INT8, zero-overhead).
- M6: <100 KB (boosted NMS parameters).
- M8: 3–5 MB (INT8 temporal CNN).
- M11: <500 KB (tiny transformer).
- M3/M9 loaded on demand: 5–10 MB each, evicted when idle.

---

## 10. The single most important AI/ML design decision

If one principle must govern the entire AI/ML layer, it is this: **the models must be trained on the decoder’s actual failure modes, not on generic image quality.** A super-resolution model that produces beautiful images but changes a symbol is worse than no model at all. A decodability oracle that predicts “image quality” instead of “decodability by this specific decoder” is useless. Every model in this portfolio is coupled to the **end-to-end symbol error rate and frame error rate** through a differentiable or reinforcement-learning path:

- M3 and M9 use a **decode-in-the-loop loss** (M5’s output).
- M6 is trained on **M5’s confusion matrix**, not on AWGN.
- M7 is rewarded on **actual completion time**.
- M13 is trained **adversarially against the decoders**.

This coupling is what turns a collection of models into a **co-designed cognitive optical stack**. The leap is not any single architecture; it is the closed loop between perception, decoding, and channel adaptation that no existing optical code possesses.

"Provide the README for the project"


# PRISM

**Progressive Recursive Integrated Symbolic Modulation**

*An optical data transport system for the post‑QR era.*

[![Status](https://img.shields.io/badge/status-research%20%2F%20prototype-blue)]() [![Platform](https://img.shields.io/badge/platform-Android%2010%2B-green)]() [![Core](https://img.shields.io/badge/core-Rust%20%2B%20Kotlin-orange)]() [![License](https://img.shields.io/badge/license-Apache%202.0-lightgrey)]()

---

## What is PRISM?

PRISM is a next‑generation optical data transport system that moves **megabytes to gigabytes** of arbitrary binary data—including APK files, archives, documents, images, and any other file type—across an optical channel using nothing but a display or printed surface and a camera.

PRISM replaces the flat, binary, single‑image QR paradigm with:

- **Aperiodic tiling** (chiral monotile coding) for gridless, occlusion‑resistant symbols.
- **Recursive fountain lattices** for hierarchical, loss‑tolerant data layering.
- **Chromatic–Geometric–Glyph (CGG) alphabets** for dense, multi‑dimensional symbol modulation using shapes, colours, numbers, and letters.
- **Hybrid error correction** (non‑binary LDPC + RaptorQ/LT fountain codes) for both within‑frame and across‑frame robustness.
- **A purpose‑built AI/ML cognitive plane** that perceives the channel, predicts decodability, restores degraded captures, and adapts redundancy in real time.

PRISM ships as **two transport modes** that share a single codec core: a **static printable code** and a **kinetic rotating mass**.

---

## The Two Systems

### System A — Static Printable Code (SPC)

A **Progressive Static Mosaic**: a printed page containing a coarse metadata band readable by older hardware and fine fountain‑coded payload tiles readable by high‑resolution scanners.

| Property | Value |
|---|---|
| Capacity per A4 page | 200–500 KB |
| Print resolution | 600–1200 dpi |
| Compatibility | Old phones read the metadata band; modern scanners recover the full payload |
| Use cases | Archival, air‑gapped distribution, print‑only environments, offline publishing |

See [`docs/static-mode.md`](docs/static-mode.md) for the full page architecture, tile layout, and print specifications.

### System B — Dynamic Rotating Mass (DRM)

A **Face‑Rate‑Matched Kinetic Fountain**: a rotating three‑dimensional object with data‑bearing faces, deliberately spun slowly enough that even an 8 fps camera captures every face once per revolution.

| Property | Value |
|---|---|
| Face presentation rate | 8 fps (matched to slowest supported camera) |
| Effective throughput | ~46 KB/s at 8 fps, ~150 KB/s at 15 fps, ~300+ KB/s at 30 fps (with 7.5 KB faces) |
| Effective throughput (dense faces) | Up to ~720 KB/s at 30 fps with 24 KB faces |
| Use cases | Installation art, interactive exhibits, physical data sculptures, rapid large‑file transfer |

See [`docs/kinetic-mode.md`](docs/kinetic-mode.md) for the physical design, kinematic rules, and motor control.

---

## Key Innovations

| Innovation | What it does | Why it matters |
|---|---|---|
| **Chiral Monotile Coding (CMC)** | Uses Spectre aperiodic monotile orientations, chirality, and cluster levels as independent symbol dimensions | Occlusion tolerance with a tunable, hierarchical code layer |
| **Recursive Fountain Lattice (RFL)** | Hierarchical fountain layers with successive refinement and structural UEP | Structural unequal error protection; graceful degradation |
| **Perceptual Chromatic Lattice (PCL)** | CIECAM02 colour alphabet with per‑frame calibration and adaptive palette size | Colour multiplexing that survives real screen‑camera channels |
| **Channel‑Optimized Geometric Alphabet (COGA)** | Differentiable‑rendering‑optimized shape alphabet | Shapes maximally distinguishable under blur, not just visually distinct |
| **Hierarchical Alphabet Partitioning + MLC (HAP‑MLC)** | Set partitioning over the CGG alphabet with multi‑level coding | Graceful degradation across channel conditions |
| **Symbol‑Metric Hybrid Coding (SMHC)** | NB‑LDPC inner + fountain outer, both symbol‑native with soft metrics | Corrects optical symbol errors directly |
| **Visual‑Aware Fountain Coding (VAFC)** | Reliability‑weighted LT/RaptorQ degree distributions | Repair overhead goes where it matters |
| **Progressive Static Mosaic (PSM)** | Dual‑resolution printed page with coarse metadata band | Backward compatibility with old hardware |
| **Face‑Rate‑Matched Kinetic Fountain (FRM‑KF)** | Kinetic throughput dictated by the weakest camera | Guaranteed per‑face capture without synchronization |
| **PRISM Cognitive Plane** | Co‑designed AI/ML models (M1–M13) closed‑loop with the codec | Every model trained on the decoder's actual failure modes |

---

## Architecture

```
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                         ANDROID APPLICATION                          │
│  ┌────────────┐   ┌──────────────┐   ┌──────────────────────────┐   │
│  │  Kotlin UI │   │  CameraX     │   │  Motor Control (BLE)     │   │
│  │  & Flow    │   │  Capture     │   │  (Kinetic mode only)     │   │
│  └─────┬──────┘   └──────┬───────┘   └──────────┬───────────────┘   │
│        │                 │                       │                   │
│        └─────────────────┼───────────────────────┘                   │
│                          ▼                                           │
│  ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐  │
│  │                    COGNITIVE PLANE (JNI)                      │  │
│  │  M1 Oracle │ M2 Localiser │ M4 Calibrator │ M5 Classifier    │  │
│  │  M8 Tracker│ M11 Channel Transformer │ M6 Neural Decoder      │  │
│  │  M3 SR     │ M9 Deblurrer  │ M7 Fountain Optimiser            │  │
│  └──────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────────┘  │
│                             ▼                                       │
│  ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐  │
│  │              CODEC CORE (Rust via jni-rs)                     │  │
│  │  CGG Alphabet → HAP-MLC → NB-LDPC → RaptorQ/LT Fountain       │  │
│  │  CMC Tiling │ PCL Colour │ COGA Shapes │ VAFC Weighting        │  │
│  └──────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────────┘  │
│                             ▼                                       │
│  ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐  │
│  │           FILE I/O + INTEGRITY (Kotlin)                       │  │
│  │  ContentResolver │ SHA-256 Verify │ Streaming Write           │  │
│  └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘  │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

The codec core is shared across both transport modes. Only the physical transport layer (printed page vs. rotating mass) differs.

---

## The Codec Core

### Alphabet — CGG

| Mode | Symbol set | Bits per cell | Use |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Dense** | 16 shapes × 4 colours | 6 bits | Payload tiles |
| **Dense+** | 16 shapes × 8 colours | 7 bits | High‑quality print or bright display |
| **Readable** | 10 digits + 26 letters + 16 shapes | ~5.7 bits | Headers, metadata, human inspection |

### FEC stack

```
Payload bytes
   │
   ▼
HAP-MLC partition tree  (set partitioning over CGG alphabet)
   │
   ▼
Non-binary LDPC over GF(q)  (inner, symbol-native, soft metrics)
   │
   ▼
RaptorQ / LT fountain code  (outer, cross-frame erasure tolerance)
   │
   ▼
CGG symbol stream  →  CMC / PCL / COGA  →  frames or tiles
```

### Integrity

- SHA‑256 of the original file is embedded in the RFL root layer.
- The reconstructed file is verified before delivery.
- Corrupted reconstructions are discarded; the fountain decoder continues collecting packets.

See [`docs/codec.md`](docs/codec.md) for the full specification.

---

## The AI/ML Pipeline

PRISM's cognitive plane is a closed‑loop set of **13 purpose‑built models** (M1–M13). Every model is trained on the decoder's actual failure modes, not on generic image quality.

| # | Model | Role | Deployment |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1 | **Decodability Oracle** | Predict if a frame is worth decoding | CPU / NNAPI |
| M2 | **Tile Localiser** | Detect and rectify code regions | GPU / NNAPI |
| M3 | **Super‑Resolution Restorer** | Upscale low‑resolution captures | GPU (on demand) |
| M4 | **Chromatic Calibrator** | Learn per‑frame colour transform from pilots | CPU |
| M5 | **Shape–Colour Classifier** | Map tiles to soft symbol probabilities | Zero‑overhead CPU |
| M6 | **Neural Inner Decoder** | Boosted NMS / message‑passing transformer for NB‑LDPC | CPU / DSP |
| M7 | **Fountain Degree Optimiser** | Choose LT/RaptorQ distributions for the current channel | On‑device MLP or lookup |
| M8 | **Face Tracker & Rotation Estimator** | Track the rotating mass, estimate phase and blur | GPU / NNAPI |
| M9 | **Motion Deblurrer** | Remove rotation and rolling‑shutter blur | GPU (on demand) |
| M10 | **Motor Synchroniser** | Adapt rotation speed to receiver fps | MCU (BLE bridge) |
| M11 | **Channel State Transformer** | Global channel embedding feeding M1–M10 | CPU |
| M12 | **Print Defect Inspector** | Classify printed mosaics before distribution | Cloud / edge GPU |
| M13 | **Synthetic Channel Generator** | Adversarial training engine for all other models | Training only |

See [`docs/ai-ml.md`](docs/ai-ml.md) for full architectures, training signals, and deployment targets.

### Training strategy

1. **Synthetic pre‑training** on the M13 differentiable channel generator.
2. **Adversarial co‑training** in a minimax loop with the channel generator.
3. **Real‑data fine‑tuning** with knowledge distillation and federated learning.
4. **Continual on‑device adaptation** via unsupervised calibration (M4, M11).

---

## Performance Targets

| Scenario | Camera | Throughput | Time for 10 MB | Time for 1 GB |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Kinetic, 7.5 KB faces | 8 fps | ~46 KB/s | ~3.6 min | ~6 hours |
| Kinetic, 7.5 KB faces | 30 fps | ~173 KB/s | ~1 min | ~1.6 hours |
| Kinetic, 24 KB faces | 30 fps | ~554 KB/s | ~18 s | ~30 min |
| Static, A4 mosaic | Scanner | — | ~40 pages | ~4000 pages |

Kinetic mode is the practical path to megabyte‑scale transfers with older hardware. Static mode is for archival and air‑gapped use.

---

## Getting Started

### Requirements

- **Android 10+** (API 29) for the scanning application.
- **Kotlin 1.9+**, **Gradle 8+**, **Android Studio Hedgehog+**.
- **Rust 1.75+** with `jni-rs` and Android NDK cross‑compilation toolchain.
- **CMake 3.22+** for native build integration.
- A physical device is strongly recommended for camera and performance testing.

### Build the Android app

```bash
git clone https://github.com/prism-ot/prism.git
cd prism/android
./gradlew :app:assembleDebug
adb install -r app/build/outputs/apk/debug/app-debug.apk
```

### Build the Rust codec core

```bash
cd prism/core
cargo build --release --target aarch64-linux-android
cargo build --release --target armv7-linux-androideabi
```

The JNI bindings and prebuilt `.so` files are packaged automatically by the Gradle build via the `cargo-ndk` plugin.

### Generate a static mosaic

```bash
prism encode --mode static --input archive.zip --pages 8 --dpi 600 --output mosaic/
```

### Generate a kinetic face set

```bash
prism encode --mode kinetic --input archive.zip --faces 32 --face-bytes 24576 --output faces/
```

### Scan (from the app)

1. Launch PRISM.
2. Select **Static Scan** or **Kinetic Scan**.
3. Point the camera at the page or rotating mass.
4. The app displays decoded tile/face progress and reconstructs the file when enough packets are collected.
5. The reconstructed file's SHA‑256 is verified before it is exposed to the user.

---

## Roadmap

### Phase 1 — Production baseline (0–6 months)
- Multi‑frame QR + RaptorQ transport, native ZXing decoder pool.
- Static mode v1 with coarse metadata band.
- Kinetic mode v1 with fixed face rate and 8 KB faces.
- SHA‑256 integrity verification.
- M1, M4, M5, M6 (classical baseline).

### Phase 2 — Differentiation (6–12 months)
- CMC aperiodic tiling; CGG dense alphabet.
- HAP‑MLC and SMHC hybrid FEC.
- PCL colour calibration and COGA shape optimisation.
- M2, M3, M8, M9, M11.
- Adaptive face‑rate matching.

### Phase 3 — Leap (12–24 months)
- Full RFL recursive fountain lattice.
- VAFC weighted fountain coding.
- M7, M10, M12, M13.
- Multi‑page static mosaics with cross‑page fountain continuity.
- Publication of the CMC and RFL specifications.

See [`ROADMAP.md`](ROADMAP.md) for the detailed milestone breakdown.

---

## Research Foundations

PRISM is built on and extends:

- **Aperiodic tiling codes**: SSMC (Penrose tiling optical codes), Hat/Spectre aperiodic monotiles, and aperiodic quantum error‑correcting codes.
- **High‑density optical codes**: LIBCIMBAR, JAB Code (ISO/IEC 23634), HD InfoCode, PaperBack, NestedDot, 4SQR, Hexatess, HCCB/HC8C.
- **Screen‑camera communication**: CCRCode, ERSCC, AirFerry, Decimen, QRFerry, ChromaCode.
- **Fountain coding**: RaptorQ (RFC 6330), LT codes, weighted/unequal‑error‑protection fountain variants.
- **Neural decoding**: boosted neural min‑sum, message‑passing transformers, zero‑overhead ANN barcode decoders, YOLO‑Barcode, 4SQR‑CodeScanner, Mosaic‑SR, GANOCCAS.
- **On‑device ML**: LiteRT, NNAPI, quantisation‑aware training, INT8 zero‑overhead inference.
- **Mobile platform**: Android CameraX, `STRATEGY_KEEP_ONLY_LATEST`, `jni-rs`, `rxing`, `qrose`, `qrcode-kotlin`.

Full citations are maintained in [`docs/research-notes.md`](docs/research-notes.md).

---

## Contributing

PRISM is in active research and early prototyping. Contributions are welcome in the following areas:

- **Aperiodic tiling implementations** (Spectre, Hat, Penrose encoders/decoders).
- **Non‑binary LDPC decoders** with soft symbol metrics.
- **Fountain code optimisations** for structured erasure channels.
- **On‑device ML deployment** (LiteRT, NNAPI, zero‑overhead inference).
- **Print pipeline tooling** (colour calibration, defect inspection, DPI profiling).
- **Real‑world capture datasets** for the synthetic channel generator.

Please open an issue before submitting a large PR, so we can align on the design.

See [`CONTRIBUTING.md`](CONTRIBUTING.md) for coding standards, test requirements, and the review process.

# PRISM Android App — Implementation Plan

Before the code, here's the complete batch plan so you know what's coming and can ask for adjustments between batches:

| Batch | Contents | Rough size |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | Project scaffold: Gradle files, version catalog, manifest, ProGuard | ~250 lines |
| **2** | Application class, Hilt DI, preferences, constants, shared data models | ~500 lines |
| **3** | ML layer: JNI bridge, 13 model wrappers, ModelLoader | ~800 lines |
| **4** | Codec layer: CGG, channel state, fountain, RaptorQ, PrismCodec | ~700 lines |
| **5** | I/O + transfer: FileHandler, SHA-256, TransferSession, results | ~500 lines |
| **6** | Capture pipeline: CameraX controller, frame analyzer, foreground service | ~600 lines |
| **7** | Motor/BLE: BleMotorService, MotorController, MotorSynchroniser | ~400 lines |
| **8** | Session orchestration: ScanSession, Static, Kinetic | ~500 lines |
| **9** | UI theme, navigation, MainActivity, ViewModels | ~600 lines |
| **10** | UI screens: Home, StaticScan, KineticScan, Encode, Settings | ~900 lines |
| **11** | UI components + resources (strings, colors, themes, dimens) | ~600 lines |

That's ~6,500 lines across 11 batches. Batch 12 (tests) can follow if you want it. I'll deliver one batch per turn; say "next" to continue.

## 1.6 `app/proguard-rules.pro`

```proguard
# ---- PRISM JNI bridge ----
-keep class com.prism.ot.ml.** { *; }
-keepclasseswithmembernames class com.prism.ot.ml.** {
    native <methods>;
}

# Annotation used to mark classes/members referenced from Rust via JNI
-keep @com.prism.ot.ml.KeepForJni class * { *; }
-keep @com.prism.ot.ml.KeepForJni interface * { *; }

# Rust loads these reflective call sites by name
-keepclassmembers class com.prism.ot.codec.** {
    <init>(...);
    *;
}

# ---- Kotlin serialization ----
-keepattributes *Annotation*, InnerClasses
-dontnote kotlinx.serialization.**
-keepclassmembers class kotlinx.serialization.json.** {
    *** Companion;
}
-keepclasseswithmembers class kotlinx.serialization.json.** {
    kotlinx.serialization.KSerializer serializer(...);
}
-keep,includedescriptorclasses class com.prism.ot.**$$serializer { *; }
-keepclassmembers class com.prism.ot.** {
    *** Companion;
}
-keepclasseswithmembers class com.prism.ot.** {
    kotlinx.serialization.KSerializer serializer(...);
}

# ---- Hilt / Dagger ----
-keep,allowobfuscation,allowshrinking interface dagger.hilt.internal.GeneratedComponent
-keep class dagger.hilt.** { *; }

# ---- CameraX ----
-keep class androidx.camera.** { *; }
-dontwarn androidx.camera.**

# ---- Okio ----
-dontwarn okio.**

# ---- Enums used by serialization in transfer sessions ----
-keepclassmembers enum com.prism.ot.** {
    public static **[] values();
    public static ** valueOf(java.lang.String);
}

# PRISM Android

The scanning and encoding application for the PRISM optical transport system.

See the root `README.md` for full project documentation.

## Build

```bash
./gradlew :app:assembleDebug
```

## Native dependencies

Prebuilt Rust `.so` libraries must be present under `app/src/main/jniLibs/{arm64-v8a,armeabi-v7a}/`.

ML model assets must be present under `app/src/main/assets/models/`.

Both are intentionally omitted from VCS; see `tools/fetch_native.sh` and `tools/fetch_models.sh`.

## 2.1 `PrismApplication.kt`

```kotlin
package com.prism.ot

import android.app.Application
import android.util.Log
import com.prism.ot.core.PrismLogger
import com.prism.ot.ml.MlBridge
import dagger.hilt.android.HiltAndroidApp
import javax.inject.Inject

/**
 * Process-wide application entry point.
 *
 * Responsibilities:
 *  - Initialize Hilt graph.
 *  - Load the native Rust codec core once and fail fast if the .so is missing.
 *  - Warm ML model handles lazily via [MlBridge] but only *validate* their presence here.
 *  - Install an uncaught exception handler that emits a diagnostic log so we can
 *    correlate crashes against the native core version.
 */
@HiltAndroidApp
class PrismApplication : Application() {

    @Inject lateinit var logger: PrismLogger
    @Inject lateinit var mlBridge: MlBridge

    override fun onCreate() {
        super.onCreate()
        installCrashHandler()
        validateNativeCore()
        validateModelAssets()
    }

    private fun installCrashHandler() {
        val prior = Thread.getDefaultUncaughtExceptionHandler()
        Thread.setDefaultUncaughtExceptionHandler { thread, throwable ->
            try {
                logger.e(
                    TAG,
                    "Uncaught exception on ${thread.name}: ${throwable.javaClass.simpleName}",
                    throwable
                )
            } finally {
                prior?.uncaughtException(thread, throwable)
            }
        }
    }

    private fun validateNativeCore() {
        runCatching {
            val version = mlBridge.nativeVersion()
            logger.i(TAG, "Native PRISM core loaded, version=$version")
        }.onFailure {
            // Not fatal: the app can still launch and show a clear error UI.
            // The codec layer will surface CodecError.NativeLibraryMissing.
            logger.e(TAG, "Native PRISM core failed to load", it)
        }
    }

    private fun validateModelAssets() {
        runCatching {
            val missing = mlBridge.listMissingModelAssets()
            if (missing.isNotEmpty()) {
                logger.w(TAG, "Missing ML model assets: $missing")
            } else {
                logger.i(TAG, "All ML model assets present")
            }
        }.onFailure { Log.w(TAG, "Model asset validation failed", it) }
    }

    companion object {
        private const val TAG = "PrismApplication"
    }
}
```

---

## 2.2 `core/PrismLogger.kt`

```kotlin
package com.prism.ot.core

import android.util.Log
import javax.inject.Inject
import javax.inject.Singleton

/**
 * Application-wide logging façade. Behind this interface we can later
 * swap Logcat for file logging, remote telemetry, or an in-memory ring
 * buffer surfaced in a debug screen.
 */
interface PrismLogger {
    fun v(tag: String, message: String)
    fun d(tag: String, message: String)
    fun i(tag: String, message: String)
    fun w(tag: String, message: String, throwable: Throwable? = null)
    fun e(tag: String, message: String, throwable: Throwable? = null)
}

@Singleton
class LogcatLogger @Inject constructor() : PrismLogger {

    private val enabled = Log.isLoggable("Prism", Log.DEBUG)
    private val verboseEnabled = Log.isLoggable("Prism", Log.VERBOSE)

    override fun v(tag: String, message: String) {
        if (verboseEnabled) Log.v(tag, message)
    }

    override fun d(tag: String, message: String) {
        if (enabled) Log.d(tag, message)
    }

    override fun i(tag: String, message: String) = Log.i(tag, message).let { }

    override fun w(tag: String, message: String, throwable: Throwable?) {
        if (throwable != null) Log.w(tag, message, throwable) else Log.w(tag, message)
    }

    override fun e(tag: String, message: String, throwable: Throwable?) {
        if (throwable != null) Log.e(tag, message, throwable) else Log.e(tag, message)
    }
}
```

---

## 2.3 `core/PrismResult.kt`

```kotlin
package com.prism.ot.core

/**
 * Minimal typed Result to avoid the checked-exception ceremony of Kotlin and
 * to make transfer outcomes explicit in the type system.
 *
 * Kotlin's built-in `Result<T>` is a good choice for fine-grained control
 * flow, but transfer operations span hours and need to be serialisable and
 * observed across coroutines, hence a sealed class.
 */
sealed class PrismResult<out T> {

    data class Success<out T>(val value: T) : PrismResult<T>()

    data class Failure(val error: PrismError, val cause: Throwable? = null) :
        PrismResult<Nothing>()

    inline fun onSuccess(block: (T) -> Unit): PrismResult<T> {
        if (this is Success) block(value)
        return this
    }

    inline fun onFailure(block: (PrismError, Throwable?) -> Unit): PrismResult<T> {
        if (this is Failure) block(error, cause)
        return this
    }

    fun getOrNull(): T? = (this as? Success)?.value

    fun getOrThrow(): T = when (this) {
        is Success -> value
        is Failure -> throw PrismException(error, cause)
    }
}

class PrismException(
    val error: PrismError,
    cause: Throwable? = null
) : Exception(error.userMessage, cause)
```

---

## 2.4 `core/PrismError.kt`

```kotlin
package com.prism.ot.core

/**
 * Exhaustive error taxonomy. Every layer returns one of these rather than
 * raw exceptions so the UI can render an actionable message and analytics
 * can bucket failures consistently.
 */
sealed class PrismError(val userMessage: String, val code: String) {

    // ---- Camera / capture ----
    data object CameraPermissionDenied :
        PrismError("Camera permission is required to scan.", "CAM_PERM")

    data object CameraUnavailable :
        PrismError("Camera is not available or in use by another app.", "CAM_UNAVAIL")

    data class CameraInitializationFailed(val detail: String) :
        PrismError("Camera failed to start: $detail", "CAM_INIT")

    data class CameraFrameDropped(val count: Long) :
        PrismError("$count frames were dropped by the camera.", "CAM_DROP")

    // ---- Codec / symbol ----
    data object NativeLibraryMissing :
        PrismError("PRISM native core is missing from this build.", "CODEC_NATIVE")

    data class CodecInitializationFailed(val detail: String) :
        PrismError("Codec initialisation failed: $detail", "CODEC_INIT")

    data class InvalidTile(val reason: String) :
        PrismError("A code tile could not be decoded: $reason", "CODEC_TILE")

    data class UnsupportedSymbolVersion(val version: Int) :
        PrismError("This code uses a newer PRISM format (v$version).", "CODEC_VERSION")

    data object NoTilesDetected :
        PrismError("No PRISM code was detected in the frame.", "CODEC_NOTILES")

    // ---- Fountain / transfer ----
    data class FountainDecodeFailed(val reason: String) :
        PrismError("Fountain decoding stalled: $reason", "FNT_STALL")

    data class IntegrityMismatch(val expectedSha256: String, val actualSha256: String) :
        PrismError(
            "File integrity check failed. The transfer was corrupted and discarded.",
            "FNT_HASH"
        )

    data class InsufficientPackets(val collected: Int, val required: Int) :
        PrismError(
            "Collected $collected of $required packets. Keep scanning.",
            "FNT_PACKETS"
        )

    data object TransferCancelled :
        PrismError("Transfer was cancelled.", "FNT_CANCEL")

    // ---- I/O ----
    data class FileReadFailed(val detail: String) :
        PrismError("Could not read the source file: $detail", "IO_READ")

    data class FileWriteFailed(val detail: String) :
        PrismError("Could not write the received file: $detail", "IO_WRITE")

    data object InsufficientStorage :
        PrismError("Not enough free storage for this transfer.", "IO_STORAGE")

    // ---- Motor / kinetic ----
    data object BluetoothUnavailable :
        PrismError("Bluetooth is required to control the rotating mass.", "MOTOR_BT")

    data class MotorConnectionFailed(val detail: String) :
        PrismError("Could not connect to the rotating mass: $detail", "MOTOR_CONN")

    data class MotorCommandFailed(val detail: String) :
        PrismError("Motor command failed: $detail", "MOTOR_CMD")

    // ---- ML ----
    data class ModelLoadFailed(val modelId: String, val detail: String) :
        PrismError("AI model '$modelId' failed to load: $detail", "ML_LOAD")

    data class ModelInferenceFailed(val modelId: String, val detail: String) :
        PrismError("AI model '$modelId' inference error: $detail", "ML_INFER")

    // ---- UI / flow ----
    data object UserAborted :
        PrismError("Operation cancelled by the user.", "USER_ABORT")

    data class Unexpected(val detail: String) :
        PrismError("Unexpected error: $detail", "UNEXPECTED")
}
```

## 2.5 `core/PrismConstants.kt`

```kotlin
package com.prism.ot.core

/**
 * Single source of truth for tunables. Any constant that influences the
 * on-disk format, wire format, or fountain code semantics is frozen here
 * and versioned via [FORMAT_VERSION].
 */
object PrismConstants {

    // ---- Format ----
    const val FORMAT_VERSION: Int = 1
    const val MIN_SUPPORTED_FORMAT_VERSION: Int = 1

    // ---- Static mode ----
    const val STATIC_TILE_PIXELS: Int = 1024
    const val STATIC_TILE_BYTES: Int = 7_500              // conservative Dense alphabet
    const val STATIC_TILE_BYTES_DENSE_PLUS: Int = 12_000
    const val STATIC_DEFAULT_DPI: Int = 600
    const val STATIC_MAX_DPI: Int = 1200
    const val STATIC_TILES_PER_PAGE: Int = 30
    const val STATIC_METADATA_BAND_HEIGHT_PX: Int = 220
    const val STATIC_PAGE_A4_WIDTH_MM: Float = 210f
    const val STATIC_PAGE_A4_HEIGHT_MM: Float = 297f

    // ---- Kinetic mode ----
    const val KINETIC_MIN_FACE_BYTES: Int = 4_096
    const val KINETIC_DEFAULT_FACE_BYTES: Int = 7_500
    const val KINETIC_MAX_FACE_BYTES: Int = 24_576
    const val KINETIC_MIN_FACES: Int = 8
    const val KINETIC_DEFAULT_FACES: Int = 16
    const val KINETIC_MAX_FACES: Int = 32
    const val KINETIC_FACE_RATE_TOLERANCE: Float = 0.85f    // must stay below min fps
    const val KINETIC_MIN_CAMERA_FPS: Int = 8
    const val KINETIC_MAX_CAMERA_FPS: Int = 120
    const val KINETIC_DEFAULT_ROTATION_HZ: Float = 0.25f
    const val KINETIC_MAX_ROTATION_HZ: Float = 1.0f

    // ---- Fountain / transfer ----
    const val FOUNTAIN_SEGMENT_BYTES: Int = 32 * 1024 * 1024       // 32 MiB
    const val FOUNTAIN_TARGET_OVERHEAD: Float = 1.30f
    const val FOUNTAIN_MIN_OVERHEAD: Float = 1.10f
    const val FOUNTAIN_MAX_OVERHEAD: Float = 1.80f
    const val FOUNTAIN_PACKET_HEADER_BYTES: Int = 24

    // ---- Camera ----
    const val TARGET_CAPTURE_FPS: Int = 60
    const val FALLBACK_CAPTURE_FPS: Int = 30
    const val ANALYSIS_TARGET_WIDTH: Int = 1280
    const val ANALYSIS_TARGET_HEIGHT: Int = 720

    // ---- ML ----
    const val MODEL_INPUT_SIZE: Int = 224
    const val MODEL_TILE_INPUT_SIZE: Int = 32
    const val MODEL_DECISION_THRESHOLD: Float = 0.55f
    const val MODEL_TEMPERATURE: Float = 1.20f             // softmax temperature for M5
    const val CHANNEL_STATE_HISTORY: Int = 16
    const val SYMBOL_ALPHABET_SIZE_DENSE: Int = 64          // 16 shapes × 4 colours
    const val SYMBOL_ALPHABET_SIZE_DENSE_PLUS: Int = 128    // 16 shapes × 8 colours
    const val SYMBOL_ALPHABET_SIZE_READABLE: Int = 52

    // ---- Integrity ----
    const val SHA256_HEX_LENGTH: Int = 64
    const val SHA256_BUFFER_BYTES: Int = 64 * 1024

    // ---- File handling ----
    const val RECEIVED_DIR: String = "received"
    const val TEMP_DIR: String = "incoming"
    const val MAX_CONCURRENT_DECODERS: Int = 3
    const val PROGRESS_EMIT_INTERVAL_MS: Long = 150L

    // ---- Session ----
    const val SESSION_ID_BYTES: Int = 16
    const val SESSION_TIMEOUT_MS: Long = 30L * 60L * 1000L      // 30 minutes
    const val SESSION_KEEP_ALIVE_MS: Long = 5L * 60L * 1000L    // 5 minutes

    // ---- BLE motor ----
    const val MOTOR_SERVICE_UUID: String = "0000prism-0000-1000-8000-00805f9b34fb"
    const val MOTOR_CHAR_SPEED_UUID: String = "0000pris1-0000-1000-8000-00805f9b34fb"
    const val MOTOR_CHAR_TELEMETRY_UUID: String = "0000pris2-0000-1000-8000-00805f9b34fb"
    const val MOTOR_RECONNECT_ATTEMPTS: Int = 3
}

## 2.6 `data/model/FileMetadata.kt`

```kotlin
package com.prism.ot.data.model

import kotlinx.serialization.Serializable

/**
 * Immutable description of a payload being sent or received.
 *
 * This header travels in the fountain code's root block (RFL level 0) so the
 * receiver can display progress, validate SHA-256, and reconstruct the file
 * even before any payload blocks are decoded.
 */
@Serializable
data class FileMetadata(
    val fileName: String,
    val mimeType: String,
    val sizeBytes: Long,
    val sha256Hex: String,
    val segmentSizeBytes: Int,
    val segmentCount: Int,
    val formatVersion: Int,
    val createdAtEpochMs: Long,
    val appPackageName: String? = null,  // for APK payloads
    val appVersionName: String? = null,
) {
    init {
        require(sizeBytes >= 0) { "sizeBytes must be non-negative" }
        require(segmentCount >= 0) { "segmentCount must be non-negative" }
        require(sha256Hex.length == 64) { "sha256Hex must be 64 hex chars" }
    }

    val totalPacketsRequired: Int
        get() = ((segmentCount * segmentSizeBytes).toDouble() * 1.0)
            .toInt() + segmentCount  // 1 metadata packet per segment minimum
}
```

---

## 2.7 `data/model/TransferMode.kt`

```kotlin
package com.prism.ot.data.model

import kotlinx.serialization.Serializable

/**
 * Which physical transport is being used for the current transfer.
 * [FALLBACK] is used when the app detects that the custom PRISM symbol
 * cannot be reliably decoded on the current device and silently falls back
 * to standard QR framing with fountain coding.
 */
@Serializable
enum class TransferMode {
    STATIC,
    KINETIC,
    FALLBACK;

    val displayName: String
        get() = when (this) {
            STATIC -> "Static"
            KINETIC -> "Kinetic"
            FALLBACK -> "Fallback (QR)"
        }
}
```

---

## 2.8 `data/model/TransferProgress.kt`

```kotlin
package com.prism.ot.data.model

/**
 * Snapshot of an in-flight transfer. Emitted to the UI at
 * [com.prism.ot.core.PrismConstants.PROGRESS_EMIT_INTERVAL_MS] intervals.
 *
 * Never emit per-frame; batch aggregate updates to preserve battery.
 */
data class TransferProgress(
    val sessionId: String,
    val mode: TransferMode,
    val bytesReceived: Long,
    val bytesTotal: Long,
    val packetsCollected: Int,
    val packetsRequired: Int,
    val framesProcessed: Long,
    val framesDecoded: Long,
    val tilesRejected: Long,
    val elapsedMillis: Long,
    val currentThroughputBps: Long,
    val channelState: ChannelState,
) {
    val fractionComplete: Float
        get() = if (bytesTotal <= 0L) 0f
        else (bytesReceived.toDouble() / bytesTotal.toDouble()).toFloat().coerceIn(0f, 1f)

    val decodeSuccessRate: Float
        get() = if (framesProcessed == 0L) 0f
        else (framesDecoded.toDouble() / framesProcessed.toDouble()).toFloat()

    val etaMillis: Long
        get() {
            if (currentThroughputBps <= 0L) return -1L
            val remaining = (bytesTotal - bytesReceived).coerceAtLeast(0L)
            return (remaining * 1000L) / currentThroughputBps
        }
}
```

---

## 2.9 `data/model/ChannelState.kt`

```kotlin
package com.prism.ot.data.model

/**
 * Discrete snapshot of the optical channel, produced by the M11 Channel State
 * Transformer and fed into M3, M6, M7, M10, and M1. Immutable so it can be
 * passed across coroutine boundaries without locks.
 */
data class ChannelState(
    /** Estimated Gaussian blur sigma in pixels on the analysis frame. */
    val blurSigmaPx: Float = 0f,
    /** Mean ΔE00 residual between pilots and intended CIELAB coordinates. */
    val colorShiftDelta: Float = 0f,
    /** Estimated perspective tilt angle in degrees. */
    val perspectiveAngleDeg: Float = 0f,
    /** Approximate signal-to-noise ratio in dB. */
    val snrDb: Float = 0f,
    /** Sliding-window erasure rate [0,1]. */
    val erasureRate: Float = 0f,
    /** Recent decode success ratio [0,1] over the last window. */
    val recentDecodeRate: Float = 0f,
    /** Estimated current frame rate of the capture pipeline. */
    val captureFps: Float = 0f,
    /** Normalised reliability [0,1]; 1.0 = pristine. */
    val overallQuality: Float = 0f,
) {
    val isDegraded: Boolean
        get() = blurSigmaPx > 2.5f ||
            colorShiftDelta > 8f ||
            erasureRate > 0.35f ||
            recentDecodeRate < 0.4f

    companion object {
        val UNKNOWN = ChannelState()

        fun fromQuality(quality: Float): ChannelState =
            ChannelState(overallQuality = quality.coerceIn(0f, 1f))
    }
}
```

---

## 2.10 `data/model/DecodedTile.kt`

```kotlin
package com.prism.ot.data.model

/**
 * Result of decoding a single spatial tile (static) or face (kinetic).
 * `packetBytes` is the fountain-encoded payload extracted from the tile.
 */
data class DecodedTile(
    val tileId: Int,
    val faceId: Int? = null,
    val packetBytes: ByteArray,
    /** Average confidence of the symbol classifier for this tile [0,1]. */
    val symbolConfidence: Float,
    /** True if the inner NB-LDPC corrected at least one symbol error. */
    val wasErrorCorrected: Boolean,
    /** Source frame timestamp for temporal ordering. */
    val capturedAtEpochMs: Long,
) {
    override fun equals(other: Any?): Boolean {
        if (this === other) return true
        if (other !is DecodedTile) return false
        return tileId == other.tileId &&
            faceId == other.faceId &&
            symbolConfidence == other.symbolConfidence &&
            wasErrorCorrected == other.wasErrorCorrected &&
            capturedAtEpochMs == other.capturedAtEpochMs &&
            packetBytes.contentEquals(other.packetBytes)
    }

    override fun hashCode(): Int {
        var result = tileId
        result = 31 * result + (faceId ?: 0)
        result = 31 * result + packetBytes.contentHashCode()
        result = 31 * result + symbolConfidence.hashCode()
        result = 31 * result + wasErrorCorrected.hashCode()
        result = 31 * result + capturedAtEpochMs.hashCode()
        return result
    }
}
```

---

## 2.11 `data/model/FountainPacket.kt`

```kotlin
package com.prism.ot.data.model

/**
 * A single fountain-encoded packet extracted from a decoded tile.
 * [sequence] is the encoded-symbol index (0-based). [seed] drives
 * the deterministic LT/RaptorQ neighbour selection used by the decoder.
 */
data class FountainPacket(
    val segmentIndex: Int,
    val sequence: Int,
    val seed: Long,
    val degree: Int,
    val payload: ByteArray,
) {
    val size: Int get() = payload.size + HEADER_BYTES

    override fun equals(other: Any?): Boolean {
        if (this === other) return true
        if (other !is FountainPacket) return false
        return segmentIndex == other.segmentIndex &&
            sequence == other.sequence &&
            seed == other.seed &&
            degree == other.degree &&
            payload.contentEquals(other.payload)
    }

    override fun hashCode(): Int {
        var result = segmentIndex
        result = 31 * result + sequence
        result = 31 * result + seed.hashCode()
        result = 31 * result + degree
        result = 31 * result + payload.contentHashCode()
        return result
    }

    companion object {
        const val HEADER_BYTES: Int = 24
    }
}
```

import android.net.Uri

/**

sealed class TransferResult {

        val framesConsumed: Long,
        val mode: TransferMode,
    ) : TransferResult()

    data class Cancelled(
        val sessionId: String,
        val bytesReceived: Long,
        val bytesTotal: Long,
    ) : TransferResult()

    data class Failed(
        val error: com.prism.ot.core.PrismError,
        val partialBytesReceived: Long,

    ) : TransferResult()
}

## 2.14 `ml/KeepForJni.kt`

```kotlin
package com.prism.ot.ml

/**
 * Marker annotation. Any class or member annotated with this is referenced
 * by name from the Rust JNI bridge and must therefore survive R8/ProGuard
 * minification. The ProGuard rules in `app/proguard-rules.pro` enforce this.
 */
@Retention(AnnotationRetention.BINARY)
@Target(
    AnnotationTarget.CLASS,
    AnnotationTarget.FUNCTION,
    AnnotationTarget.PROPERTY,
    AnnotationTarget.FIELD,
    AnnotationTarget.CONSTRUCTOR
)
annotation class KeepForJni

## 2.15 `data/preferences/PrismPreferences.kt`

```kotlin
package com.prism.ot.data.preferences

import android.content.Context
import androidx.datastore.core.DataStore
import androidx.datastore.preferences.core.Preferences
import androidx.datastore.preferences.core.booleanPreferencesKey
import androidx.datastore.preferences.core.edit
import androidx.datastore.preferences.core.floatPreferencesKey
import androidx.datastore.preferences.core.intPreferencesKey
import androidx.datastore.preferences.core.longPreferencesKey
import androidx.datastore.preferences.core.stringPreferencesKey
import androidx.datastore.preferences.preferencesDataStore
import com.prism.ot.data.model.TransferMode
import kotlinx.coroutines.flow.Flow
import kotlinx.coroutines.flow.first
import kotlinx.coroutines.flow.map
import javax.inject.Inject
import javax.inject.Singleton

private val Context.prismDataStore: DataStore<Preferences> by preferencesDataStore(
    name = "prism_preferences"
)

/**
 * Persistent user and session preferences. All setters are suspend to avoid
 * accidentally doing disk I/O on the main thread.
 */
@Singleton
class PrismPreferences @Inject constructor(
    private val context: Context,
) {

    // ---- User-facing settings ----
    val preferredTransferMode: Flow<TransferMode> = context.prismDataStore.data.map {
        val raw = it[KEY_PREFERRED_MODE] ?: TransferMode.KINETIC.name
        runCatching { TransferMode.valueOf(raw) }.getOrDefault(TransferMode.KINETIC)
    }

    val autoDetectMode: Flow<Boolean> = context.prismDataStore.data.map {
        it[KEY_AUTO_DETECT_MODE] ?: true
    }

    val hapticFeedbackEnabled: Flow<Boolean> = context.prismDataStore.data.map {
        it[KEY_HAPTIC] ?: true
    }

    val soundFeedbackEnabled: Flow<Boolean> = context.prismDataStore.data.map {
        it[KEY_SOUND] ?: true
    }

    val keepScreenOn: Flow<Boolean> = context.prismDataStore.data.map {
        it[KEY_KEEP_SCREEN_ON] ?: true
    }

    val debugOverlayEnabled: Flow<Boolean> = context.prismDataStore.data.map {
        it[KEY_DEBUG_OVERLAY] ?: false
    }

    // ---- Tuning ----
    val preferredFaceBytes: Flow<Int> = context.prismDataStore.data.map {
        it[KEY_FACE_BYTES] ?: com.prism.ot.core.PrismConstants.KINETIC_DEFAULT_FACE_BYTES
    }

    val preferredFacesPerMass: Flow<Int> = context.prismDataStore.data.map {
        it[KEY_FACES] ?: com.prism.ot.core.PrismConstants.KINETIC_DEFAULT_FACES
    }

    val preferredStaticDpi: Flow<Int> = context.prismDataStore.data.map {
        it[KEY_STATIC_DPI] ?: com.prism.ot.core.PrismConstants.STATIC_DEFAULT_DPI
    }

    val preferredRotationHz: Flow<Float> = context.prismDataStore.data.map {
        it[KEY_ROTATION_HZ] ?: com.prism.ot.core.PrismConstants.KINETIC_DEFAULT_ROTATION_HZ
    }

    // ---- Derived ----
    val lastKnownCameraFps: Flow<Float> = context.prismDataStore.data.map {
        it[KEY_LAST_CAMERA_FPS] ?: 0f
    }

    val totalBytesTransferred: Flow<Long> = context.prismDataStore.data.map {
        it[KEY_TOTAL_BYTES] ?: 0L
    }

    val totalSessionsCompleted: Flow<Int> = context.prismDataStore.data.map {
        it[KEY_TOTAL_SESSIONS] ?: 0
    }

    val lastUsedMotorMac: Flow<String?> = context.prismDataStore.data.map {
        it[KEY_MOTOR_MAC]
    }

    // ---- Setters ----
    suspend fun setPreferredTransferMode(mode: TransferMode) = edit {
        it[KEY_PREFERRED_MODE] = mode.name
    }

    suspend fun setAutoDetectMode(enabled: Boolean) = edit { it[KEY_AUTO_DETECT_MODE] = enabled }
    suspend fun setHapticFeedbackEnabled(enabled: Boolean) = edit { it[KEY_HAPTIC] = enabled }
    suspend fun setSoundFeedbackEnabled(enabled: Boolean) = edit { it[KEY_SOUND] = enabled }
    suspend fun setKeepScreenOn(enabled: Boolean) = edit { it[KEY_KEEP_SCREEN_ON] = enabled }
    suspend fun setDebugOverlayEnabled(enabled: Boolean) = edit { it[KEY_DEBUG_OVERLAY] = enabled }

    suspend fun setPreferredFaceBytes(bytes: Int) = edit {
        it[KEY_FACE_BYTES] = bytes.coerceIn(
            com.prism.ot.core.PrismConstants.KINETIC_MIN_FACE_BYTES,
            com.prism.ot.core.PrismConstants.KINETIC_MAX_FACE_BYTES
        )
    }

    suspend fun setPreferredFacesPerMass(faces: Int) = edit {
        it[KEY_FACES] = faces.coerceIn(
            com.prism.ot.core.PrismConstants.KINETIC_MIN_FACES,
            com.prism.ot.core.PrismConstants.KINETIC_MAX_FACES
        )
    }

    suspend fun setPreferredStaticDpi(dpi: Int) = edit {
        it[KEY_STATIC_DPI] = dpi.coerceIn(300, com.prism.ot.core.PrismConstants.STATIC_MAX_DPI)
    }

    suspend fun setPreferredRotationHz(hz: Float) = edit {
        it[KEY_ROTATION_HZ] = hz.coerceIn(
            com.prism.ot.core.PrismConstants.KINETIC_DEFAULT_ROTATION_HZ,
            com.prism.ot.core.PrismConstants.KINETIC_MAX_ROTATION_HZ
        )
    }

    suspend fun setLastKnownCameraFps(fps: Float) = edit { it[KEY_LAST_CAMERA_FPS] = fps }
    suspend fun setLastUsedMotorMac(mac: String?) = edit { it[KEY_MOTOR_MAC] = mac }

    suspend fun recordSessionCompletion(bytesTransferred: Long) = edit {
        val sessions = (it[KEY_TOTAL_SESSIONS] ?: 0) + 1
        val total = (it[KEY_TOTAL_BYTES] ?: 0L) + bytesTransferred
        it[KEY_TOTAL_SESSIONS] = sessions
        it[KEY_TOTAL_BYTES] = total
    }

    suspend fun clearAll() = edit { it.clear() }

    suspend fun snapshot(): Map<String, Any?> = context.prismDataStore.data.first().let { prefs ->
        mapOf(
            "preferredTransferMode" to (prefs[KEY_PREFERRED_MODE] ?: "KINETIC"),
            "autoDetectMode" to (prefs[KEY_AUTO_DETECT_MODE] ?: true),
            "faceBytes" to (prefs[KEY_FACE_BYTES] ?: 7_500),
            "faces" to (prefs[KEY_FACES] ?: 16),
            "lastCameraFps" to (prefs[KEY_LAST_CAMERA_FPS] ?: 0f),
        )
    }

    private suspend inline fun edit(crossinline block: (androidx.datastore.preferences.core.MutablePreferences) -> Unit) {
        context.prismDataStore.edit { prefs -> block(prefs) }
    }

    private companion object {
        val KEY_PREFERRED_MODE = stringPreferencesKey("pref_mode")
        val KEY_AUTO_DETECT_MODE = booleanPreferencesKey("auto_detect_mode")
        val KEY_HAPTIC = booleanPreferencesKey("haptic")
        val KEY_SOUND = booleanPreferencesKey("sound")
        val KEY_KEEP_SCREEN_ON = booleanPreferencesKey("keep_screen_on")
        val KEY_DEBUG_OVERLAY = booleanPreferencesKey("debug_overlay")
        val KEY_FACE_BYTES = intPreferencesKey("face_bytes")
        val KEY_FACES = intPreferencesKey("faces")
        val KEY_STATIC_DPI = intPreferencesKey("static_dpi")
        val KEY_ROTATION_HZ = floatPreferencesKey("rotation_hz")
        val KEY_LAST_CAMERA_FPS = floatPreferencesKey("last_camera_fps")
        val KEY_TOTAL_BYTES = longPreferencesKey("total_bytes")
        val KEY_TOTAL_SESSIONS = intPreferencesKey("total_sessions")
        val KEY_MOTOR_MAC = stringPreferencesKey("motor_mac")
    }
}
```

## 2.16 `di/AppModule.kt`

```kotlin
package com.prism.ot.di

import android.content.Context
import com.prism.ot.core.LogcatLogger
import com.prism.ot.core.PrismLogger
import dagger.Module
import dagger.Provides
import dagger.hilt.InstallIn
import dagger.hilt.android.qualifiers.ApplicationContext
import dagger.hilt.components.SingletonComponent
import kotlinx.coroutines.CoroutineDispatcher
import kotlinx.coroutines.CoroutineScope
import kotlinx.coroutines.Dispatchers
import kotlinx.coroutines.SupervisorJob
import javax.inject.Qualifier
import javax.inject.Singleton

@Qualifier @Retention(AnnotationRetention.BINARY) annotation class IoDispatcher
@Qualifier @Retention(AnnotationRetention.BINARY) annotation class DefaultDispatcher
@Qualifier @Retention(AnnotationRetention.BINARY) annotation class MainDispatcher
@Qualifier @Retention(AnnotationRetention.BINARY) annotation class CodecDispatcher
@Qualifier @Retention(AnnotationRetention.BINARY) annotation class AppScope

@Module
@InstallIn(SingletonComponent::class)
object AppModule {

    @Provides @Singleton
    fun provideAppContext(@ApplicationContext ctx: Context): Context = ctx

    @Provides @Singleton
    fun providePrismLogger(impl: LogcatLogger): PrismLogger = impl

    @Provides @IoDispatcher
    fun provideIoDispatcher(): CoroutineDispatcher = Dispatchers.IO

    @Provides @DefaultDispatcher
    fun provideDefaultDispatcher(): CoroutineDispatcher = Dispatchers.Default

    @Provides @MainDispatcher
    fun provideMainDispatcher(): CoroutineDispatcher = Dispatchers.Main.immediate

    @Provides @CodecDispatcher @Singleton
    fun provideCodecDispatcher(): CoroutineDispatcher = Dispatchers.Default.limitedParallelism(2)

    @Provides @AppScope @Singleton
    fun provideAppScope(
        @DefaultDispatcher dispatcher: CoroutineDispatcher,
    ): CoroutineScope = CoroutineScope(SupervisorJob() + dispatcher)
}
```

package com.prism.ot.di

/**

## 2.20 `di/CameraModule.kt`

```kotlin
package com.prism.ot.di

import android.content.Context
import androidx.camera.lifecycle.ProcessCameraProvider
import com.prism.ot.capture.CameraController
import com.prism.ot.capture.CameraControllerImpl
import dagger.Binds
import dagger.Module
import dagger.Provides
import dagger.hilt.InstallIn
import dagger.hilt.android.qualifiers.ApplicationContext
import dagger.hilt.components.SingletonComponent
import kotlinx.coroutines.suspendCancellableCoroutine
import java.util.concurrent.ExecutorService
import java.util.concurrent.Executors
import javax.inject.Qualifier
import javax.inject.Singleton
import kotlin.coroutines.resume

@Qualifier @Retention(AnnotationRetention.BINARY) annotation class CameraExecutor

@Module
@InstallIn(SingletonComponent::class)
abstract class CameraModule {

    @Binds @Singleton
    abstract fun bindCameraController(impl: CameraControllerImpl): CameraController

    companion object {

        @Provides @Singleton
        @CameraExecutor
        fun provideCameraExecutor(): ExecutorService =
            Executors.newFixedThreadPool(3) { r ->
                Thread(r, "prism-camera").apply { priority = Thread.NORM_PRIORITY + 1 }
            }

        /**
         * Resolves the process-wide CameraProvider lazily and suspends until
         * the underlying future completes. Callers should cache the result.
         */
        @Provides @Singleton
        suspend fun provideCameraProvider(
            @ApplicationContext context: Context,
        ): ProcessCameraProvider = suspendCancellableCoroutine { cont ->
            val future = ProcessCameraProvider.getInstance(context)
            future.addListener({
                runCatching { future.get() }
                    .onSuccess { cont.resume(it) }
                    .onFailure { cont.resumeWith(Result.failure(it)) }
            }, context.mainExecutor)
            cont.invokeOnCancellation { future.cancel(true) }
        }
    }
}
```

---

**Batch 2 complete.**

What this gives you:

- **Fully typed error taxonomy** — every layer downstream can return `PrismError` cases that map to actionable UI, and analytics can bucket them by `code`.
- **`PrismResult<T>`** — sealed wrapper for operations that cross coroutine scopes.
- **All shared data models** — `FileMetadata`, `TransferProgress`, `ChannelState`, `DecodedTile`, `FountainPacket`, `TransferResult`, `FrameData`, `TransferMode`. These are what every subsequent layer emits/consumes.
- **`PrismConstants`** — one frozen tunables surface, including format version for forward compatibility.
- **`PrismPreferences`** — DataStore-backed prefs with suspend setters and the full kinetic/static tuning surface.
- **Seven Hilt modules** wired (App, ML, Codec, Io, Camera, Motor) — each binds an interface to an impl class that later batches will define. This is intentional so the compiler tells you *exactly* which piece is missing until the whole tree lands.
- **JNI protection** — `@KeepForJni` marker so R8 doesn't strip bridge-reflected members.

**Build status after this batch:** project won't compile yet (interfaces like `MlBridge`, `CodecBridge`, `PrismCodec`, `FileHandler`, `CameraController`, `MotorController` don't exist). That is expected — batches 3 through 5 fill them in.

Say **"next"** for **Batch 3 — ML layer** (JNI bridge, all 13 model wrappers, ModelLoader, soft-symbol pipeline).

# Batch 3 — ML Layer: JNI Bridge, Model Wrappers, Model Loader, Soft‑Symbol Pipeline

This batch is the cognitive plane. It provides:

- `MlBridge` (pure interface) and `MlBridgeImpl` (JNI + LiteRT orchestrator).
- A **runtime strategy** that reflects two important facts from current Android ML practice: **NNAPI is deprecated as of Android 15 and should not be a primary path**; **LiteRT with a GPU delegate and a CPU fallback ladder** is the supported direction. We therefore implement a **GPU → XNNPACK CPU → zero‑overhead native** ladder and **never call NNAPI as a first choice**.
- **Zero‑overhead inference (ZOI)** for the latency‑critical shape–colour classifier (M5) and neural inner decoder (M6), following the 4SQR‑CodeScanner approach of loading weights directly and performing optimised matrix operations without a deep‑learning runtime.
- All **13 model wrappers** (M1–M13) with explicit contracts, including the **kinetic‑specific models** (M8–M10) and the **adversarial channel generator** (M13).
- A **soft‑symbol pipeline** that produces calibrated probability vectors for the NB‑LDPC layer.
- A **JNI surface** for the Rust side that exposes ZOI models, the Spectre‑tiling template bank used by M2, and the native ML core version.

> **Design note:** the ML layer is intentionally *thin on the Kotlin side*. Anything that runs at frame rate or tile rate is either (a) delegated to LiteRT with an explicit accelerator ladder, or (b) handled by the Rust ZOI path through JNI. Kotlin owns orchestration, model lifecycle, and error mapping; it does **not** own hot loops.

---

## 3.1 `ml/MlBridge.kt`

```kotlin
package com.prism.ot.ml

import android.graphics.Bitmap
import com.prism.ot.core.PrismResult
import com.prism.ot.data.model.ChannelState
import com.prism.ot.data.model.DecodedTile
import com.prism.ot.data.model.FountainPacket
import com.prism.ot.ml.model.AperiodicTileDetection
import com.prism.ot.ml.model.DeblurResult
import com.prism.ot.ml.model.DecodabilityScore
import com.prism.ot.ml.model.FaceTrackState
import com.prism.ot.ml.model.FountainPolicy
import com.prism.ot.ml.model.MlModelId
import com.prism.ot.ml.model.ModelStatus
import com.prism.ot.ml.model.PrintDefectReport
import com.prism.ot.ml.model.SoftSymbolProbabilities
import com.prism.ot.ml.model.SuperResolutionResult

/**
 * Public façade for the PRISM cognitive plane.
 *
 * All methods are suspend because every model may need to wait for a
 * LiteRT interpreter compilation, a GPU shader warm-up, or a native
 * ZOI buffer to be mapped. Implementations must be safe to call from
 * multiple coroutines.
 *
 * Return values are always [PrismResult] so callers can branch on
 * [com.prism.ot.core.PrismError] without catching exceptions.
 */
interface MlBridge {

    // ---- Lifecycle ----
    suspend fun initialize(): PrismResult<Unit>
    fun nativeVersion(): String
    fun listMissingModelAssets(): List<String>
    fun modelStatus(id: MlModelId): ModelStatus

    // ---- M1: Decodability Oracle ----
    suspend fun scoreDecodability(frame: Bitmap): PrismResult<DecodabilityScore>

    // ---- M2: Tile Localiser ----
    suspend fun detectAperiodicTiles(frame: Bitmap): PrismResult<List<AperiodicTileDetection>>

    // ---- M3: Super-Resolution Restorer ----
    suspend fun restoreTile(
        tile: Bitmap,
        estimatedBlurSigmaPx: Float,
    ): PrismResult<SuperResolutionResult>

    // ---- M4: Chromatic Calibrator ----
    suspend fun calibrateChromatic(
        pilotPatches: List<ChromaticPilot>,
    ): PrismResult<ChromaticCalibration>

    // ---- M5: Shape–Colour Classifier (ZOI) ----
    suspend fun classifySymbols(
        rectifiedTile: Bitmap,
        calibration: ChromaticCalibration,
    ): PrismResult<SoftSymbolProbabilities>

    // ---- M6: Neural Inner Decoder ----
    suspend fun neuralLdpcDecode(
        softSymbols: SoftSymbolProbabilities,
        parityCheckMatrixId: Int,
    ): PrismResult<ByteArray>

    // ---- M7: Fountain Degree Optimiser ----
    suspend fun selectFountainPolicy(
        channelState: ChannelState,
        fileSizeBytes: Long,
    ): PrismResult<FountainPolicy>

    // ---- M8: Face Tracker & Rotation Estimator ----
    suspend fun trackRotatingMass(
        recentFrames: List<Bitmap>,
        previousState: FaceTrackState?,
    ): PrismResult<FaceTrackState>

    // ---- M9: Motion Deblurrer ----
    suspend fun deblurFace(
        faceFrame: Bitmap,
        estimatedAngularVelocityRadPerSec: Float,
        shutterNanos: Long,
    ): PrismResult<DeblurResult>

    // ---- M10: Motor Synchroniser (policy inference only) ----
    suspend fun suggestRotationHz(
        cameraFps: Float,
        currentDecodeRate: Float,
        currentBlurSigmaPx: Float,
    ): PrismResult<Float>

    // ---- M11: Channel State Transformer ----
    suspend fun updateChannelState(
        features: ChannelFeatureVector,
        history: List<ChannelState>,
    ): PrismResult<ChannelState>

    // ---- M12: Print Defect Inspector ----
    suspend fun inspectPrintedMosaic(page: Bitmap): PrismResult<PrintDefectReport>

    // ---- M13: Synthetic Channel Generator (training/QA only) ----
    suspend fun synthesizeChannelSample(
        cleanTile: Bitmap,
        targetBlurSigmaPx: Float,
        targetColorShiftDelta: Float,
        targetNoiseDb: Float,
    ): PrismResult<Bitmap>

    // ---- Utility ----
    suspend fun releaseAll()
}

/**
 * A single calibration pilot patch with its intended CIELAB coordinate.
 * The receiver knows what the sender printed/displayed; the calibrator
 * learns the transform from received to intended.
 */
data class ChromaticPilot(
    val intendedL: Float,
    val intendedA: Float,
    val intendedB: Float,
    val receivedR: Int,
    val receivedG: Int,
    val receivedB: Int,
)

/**
 * Output of M4. [transformMatrix] is a 3×4 affine matrix in CIELAB
 * (row-major, 12 floats) plus a bias row. Apply as:
 *   labOut = transformMatrix * [L, a, b, 1]^T
 */
data class ChromaticCalibration(
    val transformMatrix: FloatArray,
    val residualDeltaE00: Float,
    val activeColorCount: Int,
) {
    init {
        require(transformMatrix.size == 12) { "Expected 3x4 affine matrix" }
    }

    override fun equals(other: Any?): Boolean {
        if (this === other) return true
        if (other !is ChromaticCalibration) return false
        return transformMatrix.contentEquals(other.transformMatrix) &&
            residualDeltaE00 == other.residualDeltaE00 &&
            activeColorCount == other.activeColorCount
    }

    override fun hashCode(): Int {
        var result = transformMatrix.contentHashCode()
        result = 31 * result + residualDeltaE00.hashCode()
        result = 31 * result + activeColorCount
        return result
    }
}

/**
 * Input features for M11. Produced by the capture pipeline from the
 * last N frames: blur estimate, colour residual, SNR proxy, erasure rate.
 */
data class ChannelFeatureVector(
    val blurSigmaPx: Float,
    val colorResidualDeltaE00: Float,
    val snrProxyDb: Float,
    val recentErasureRate: Float,
    val recentDecodeRate: Float,
    val captureFps: Float,
    val perspectiveAngleDeg: Float,
    val frameTimestampNanos: Long,
)
```

## 3.2 `ml/model/MlModelId.kt`

```kotlin
package com.prism.ot.ml.model

/**
 * Canonical identifiers for every model in the PRISM cognitive plane.
 * Used for asset lookup, status reporting, and error attribution.
 */
enum class MlModelId(val assetName: String, val displayName: String) {
    M01_DECODABILITY("m01_decodability_int8.tflite", "Decodability Oracle"),
    M02_LOCALISER("m02_localiser_int8.tflite", "Tile Localiser"),
    M03_SUPER_RESOLUTION("m03_sr_fp16.tflite", "Super-Resolution Restorer"),
    M04_CALIBRATOR("m04_calibrator.bin", "Chromatic Calibrator"),
    M05_CLASSIFIER("m05_classifier_int8.bin", "Shape-Colour Classifier"),
    M06_NEURAL_LDPC("m06_neural_ldpc.bin", "Neural Inner Decoder"),
    M07_FOUNTAIN_POLICY("m07_fountain_policy_int8.tflite", "Fountain Degree Optimiser"),
    M08_FACE_TRACKER("m08_face_tracker_int8.tflite", "Face Tracker"),
    M09_DEBLURRER("m09_deblurrer_fp16.tflite", "Motion Deblurrer"),
    M10_MOTOR_POLICY("m10_motor_policy.bin", "Motor Synchroniser"),
    M11_CHANNEL_TRANSFORMER("m11_channel_transformer_int8.tflite", "Channel State Transformer"),
    M12_DEFECT_INSPECTOR("m12_defect_inspector_int8.tflite", "Print Defect Inspector"),
    M13_CHANNEL_GENERATOR("m13_channel_generator_fp16.tflite", "Synthetic Channel Generator"),
    ;

    val isZeroOverhead: Boolean
        get() = this == M04_CALIBRATOR ||
            this == M05_CLASSIFIER ||
            this == M06_NEURAL_LDPC ||
            this == M10_MOTOR_POLICY

    val isLatencyCritical: Boolean
        get() = this == M01_DECODABILITY ||
            this == M05_CLASSIFIER ||
            this == M06_NEURAL_LDPC ||
            this == M11_CHANNEL_TRANSFORMER
}
```

## 3.3 `ml/model/ModelStatus.kt`

```kotlin
package com.prism.ot.ml.model

sealed class ModelStatus {
    data object NotLoaded : ModelStatus()

    data class Loaded(
        val accelerator: Accelerator,
        val loadTimeMillis: Long,
    ) : ModelStatus()

    data class Failed(
        val reason: String,
        val recoverable: Boolean,
    ) : ModelStatus()
}

enum class Accelerator {
    GPU,
    CPU_XNNPACK,
    ZERO_OVERHEAD_NATIVE,
    CPU_REFERENCE,
    ;

    val displayName: String
        get() = when (this) {
            GPU -> "GPU"
            CPU_XNNPACK -> "CPU (XNNPACK)"
            ZERO_OVERHEAD_NATIVE -> "Native ZOI"
            CPU_REFERENCE -> "CPU (reference)"
        }
}
```
