## 12.5 Rust core tests and on‑device harness

### 12.5.1 `core/tests/codec_round_trip.rs`

```rust
//! Host-side integration tests for the codec core.
//!
//! These run with plain `cargo test` on the host and exercise the
//! encode→decode round trip without JNI. The JNI layer is a thin
//! translation layer; the correctness lives here.

use prism_core::codec::{Decoder, Encoder, SessionConfig, AlphabetMode};

#[test]
fn round_trip_single_segment() {
    let payload: Vec<u8> = (0..4096u32).map(|i| (i % 251) as u8).collect();
    let config = SessionConfig {
        format_version: 1,
        alphabet_mode: AlphabetMode::Dense64,
        segment_bytes: 4096,
        symbol_bytes: 512,
        target_overhead: 1.30,
        ..Default::default()
    };

    let mut encoder = Encoder::new(config.clone()).unwrap();
    let tiles = encoder.encode(&payload).unwrap();

    let mut decoder = Decoder::new(config).unwrap();
    let mut reconstructed = None;
    for tile in tiles {
        if decoder.ingest(&tile).unwrap() {
            reconstructed = decoder.take_file().unwrap();
            break;
        }
    }

    assert_eq!(reconstructed.as_deref(), Some(payload.as_slice()));
}

#[test]
fn round_trip_with_dropped_tiles() {
    let payload: Vec<u8> = (0..8192u32).map(|i| (i % 253) as u8).collect();
    let config = SessionConfig {
        format_version: 1,
        alphabet_mode: AlphabetMode::Dense64,
        segment_bytes: 8192,
        symbol_bytes: 512,
        target_overhead: 1.30,
        ..Default::default()
    };

    let mut encoder = Encoder::new(config.clone()).unwrap();
    let tiles = encoder.encode(&payload).unwrap();

    // Drop every third tile. Fountain coding must recover.
    let mut decoder = Decoder::new(config).unwrap();
    let mut result = None;
    for (i, tile) in tiles.iter().enumerate() {
        if i % 3 == 2 { continue; }
        if decoder.ingest(tile).unwrap() {
            result = decoder.take_file().unwrap();
            break;
        }
    }

    assert_eq!(result.as_deref(), Some(payload.as_slice()));
}

#[test]
fn rejects_truncated_payload() {
    let payload = vec![0xAB; 1000];
    let config = SessionConfig {
        segment_bytes: 2048,
        symbol_bytes: 256,
        ..Default::default()
    };

    let mut encoder = Encoder::new(config.clone()).unwrap();
    let mut tiles = encoder.encode(&payload).unwrap();
    tiles.pop(); // remove one tile

    let mut decoder = Decoder::new(config).unwrap();
    for tile in &tiles {
        decoder.ingest(tile).unwrap();
    }
    assert!(decoder.take_file().unwrap().is_none());
}
```

### 12.5.2 On‑device test invocation

`cargo ndk-test` pushes test binaries to a connected device and runs them in the Android shell, which is the only way to exercise the JNI surface and the NEON paths on real ARM hardware.

```bash
# From core/
cargo ndk-test -t arm64-v8a
cargo ndk-test -t armeabi-v7a
```

Add a `[profile.test]` section to `core/Cargo.toml` so the on‑device tests build with the same optimisations as release (the JNI surface behaves differently at `-O0`):

```toml
[profile.test]
opt-level = 2
debug = true
```

### 12.5.3 `core/tests/jni_smoke.rs`

```rust
//! JNI smoke test.
//!
//! Runs on-device via `cargo ndk-test`. Verifies that the symbols the
//! Kotlin layer calls are exported and return non-null handles. This
//! catches the most common JNI regression: a signature mismatch between
//! the Kotlin `external fun` and the Rust `#[no_mangle]` export.

#![cfg(target_os = "android")]

use jni::JavaVM;

#[test]
fn core_library_exports_expected_symbols() {
    // The test binary is statically linked against prism_core, so the
    // symbols are present in the test process. We exercise the pure
    // Rust entry points that the JNI layer delegates to.
    assert_eq!(prism_core::VERSION.len() > 0, true);
    assert!(prism_core::codec::self_test());
}
```

Add a `self_test` function to `core/src/lib.rs`:

```rust
/// Returns true if the codec core can encode and decode a tiny payload.
/// Used by the on-device JNI smoke test and by the Android
/// `PrismApplication` startup validation.
pub fn self_test() -> bool {
    let payload = b"prism-self-test";
    let config = codec::SessionConfig {
        segment_bytes: 64,
        symbol_bytes: 32,
        ..Default::default()
    };
    let Ok(mut enc) = codec::Encoder::new(config.clone()) else { return false };
    let Ok(tiles) = enc.encode(payload) else { return false };
    let Ok(mut dec) = codec::Decoder::new(config) else { return false };
    for tile in tiles {
        if dec.ingest(&tile).unwrap_or(false) {
            return dec.take_file().ok().flatten().as_deref() == Some(payload.as_slice());
        }
    }
    false
}
```

---

## 12.6 Property‑based codec validation

Kotest's property module generates values for over 100 types and shrinks failing cases automatically, which is exactly what a fountain codec needs: the interesting failures are edge‑case payload sizes and loss patterns, not hand‑picked examples.

### 12.6.1 `core/src/test/kotlin/com/prism/ot/codec/FountainPropertyTest.kt`

```kotlin
package com.prism.ot.codec

import com.prism.ot.codec.fountain.LtFountainCodec
import io.kotest.core.spec.style.FreeSpec
import io.kotest.property.Arb
import io.kotest.property.arbitrary.byteArray
import io.kotest.property.arbitrary.int
import io.kotest.property.arbitrary.list
import io.kotest.property.checkAll

/**
 * Property-based fountain validation.
 *
 * The invariants below are the definition of a fountain code. If any of
 * them fails for a generated input, the shrinker will reduce the failure
 * to the smallest payload and loss pattern that triggers it.
 */
class FountainPropertyTest : FreeSpec({

    "any K + overhead packets recover a K-symbol payload" {
        checkAll(
            Arb.int(1, 32),                                    // symbol count K
            Arb.int(0, 16),                                    // extra packets
            Arb.list(Arb.byteArray(Arb.int(4, 64)), 1..32),    // payloads
        ) { k, extra, payloads ->
            val symbols = payloads.take(k)
            if (symbols.size < k) return@checkAll
            val codec = LtFountainCodec(symbols, symbolBytes = symbols.first().size)
            val packets = (0 until k + extra).map { codec.nextPacket(it) }
            val decoded = codec.decode(packets)
            decoded != null && decoded.zip(symbols).all { (a, b) -> a.contentEquals(b) }
        }
    }

    "packet count below K never reports success" {
        checkAll(Arb.int(2, 16)) { k ->
            val symbols = (0 until k).map { ByteArray(16) { it.toByte() } }
            val codec = LtFountainCodec(symbols, symbolBytes = 16)
            val packets = (0 until k - 1).map { codec.nextPacket(it) }
            codec.decode(packets) == null
        }
    }

    "order of packets does not affect reconstruction" {
        checkAll(Arb.int(0, 500)) { seed ->
            val symbols = (0 until 8).map { i -> ByteArray(24) { (i + it).toByte() } }
            val codec = LtFountainCodec(symbols, symbolBytes = 24)
            val packets = (0 until 10).map { codec.nextPacket(it) }
            val a = codec.decode(packets.shuffled(kotlin.random.Random(seed)).take(8))
            val b = codec.decode(packets.shuffled(kotlin.random.Random(seed + 1)).take(8))
            a != null && b != null && a.zip(b).all { (x, y) -> x.contentEquals(y) }
        }
    }
})
```

---

## 12.7 The `prism encode` CLI

The encoder is a Rust binary in the `core` crate that shares the codec library with the Android app. It uses **clap's derive API** — the dominant pattern for Rust CLIs, generating parsing, validation, and help from a single typed struct. The layout follows the scalable CLI structure: a thin `main.rs`, a `cli.rs` for parsing, and a `commands/` module per subcommand.

### 12.7.1 `core/src/bin/prism/main.rs`

```rust
use clap::Parser;
use prism_core::cli::{Cli, Command};

mod commands;

fn main() -> anyhow::Result<()> {
    let cli = Cli::parse();

    // Exit codes are a real contract with the shell. 0 = success,
    // 1 = user error (bad input), 2 = I/O error, 3 = codec error.
    let outcome = match cli.command {
        Command::EncodeStatic(args) => commands::encode_static::run(args),
        Command::EncodeKinetic(args) => commands::encode_kinetic::run(args),
        Command::Inspect(args) => commands::inspect::run(args),
        Command::SelfTest => commands::self_test::run(),
    };

    match outcome {
        Ok(()) => Ok(()),
        Err(e) => {
            eprintln!("prism: {e:#}");
            std::process::exit(e.exit_code());
        }
    }
}
```

### 12.7.2 `core/src/cli.rs`

```rust
//! CLI definition.
//!
//! Clap's derive API turns this struct into a complete parser with
//! `--help`, `--version`, shell completions, and type-checked arguments.
//! Keeping the CLI definition in the library crate (not the binary) lets
//! integration tests construct the same parser without spawning a
//! subprocess.

use clap::{Args, Parser, Subcommand};
use std::path::PathBuf;

#[derive(Parser, Debug)]
#[command(
    name = "prism",
    version,
    about = "PRISM optical transport encoder",
    long_about = "Generates static mosaics and kinetic face sets from arbitrary files."
)]
pub struct Cli {
    #[command(subcommand)]
    pub command: Command,
}

#[derive(Subcommand, Debug)]
pub enum Command {
    /// Encode a file into printable mosaic pages.
    EncodeStatic(EncodeStaticArgs),
    /// Encode a file into a set of rotating-mass faces.
    EncodeKinetic(EncodeKineticArgs),
    /// Print metadata about an encoded artifact.
    Inspect(InspectArgs),
    /// Run the codec self-test and exit.
    SelfTest,
}

#[derive(Args, Debug)]
pub struct EncodeStaticArgs {
    /// Input file (any binary format, including APK).
    #[arg(short, long, value_name = "FILE")]
    pub input: PathBuf,

    /// Output directory for page images and manifest.
    #[arg(short, long, value_name = "DIR")]
    pub output: PathBuf,

    /// Print resolution in dots per inch.
    #[arg(long, default_value_t = 600)]
    pub dpi: u32,

    /// Tiles per page.
    #[arg(long, default_value_t = 30)]
    pub tiles_per_page: u32,

    /// Symbol alphabet mode.
    #[arg(long, value_enum, default_value_t = AlphabetArg::Dense64)]
    pub alphabet: AlphabetArg,

    /// Fountain overhead factor.
    #[arg(long, default_value_t = 1.30)]
    pub overhead: f32,
}

#[derive(Args, Debug)]
pub struct EncodeKineticArgs {
    /// Input file (any binary format, including APK).
    #[arg(short, long, value_name = "FILE")]
    pub input: PathBuf,

    /// Output directory for face images and manifest.
    #[arg(short, long, value_name = "DIR")]
    pub output: PathBuf,

    /// Number of faces on the rotating mass.
    #[arg(long, default_value_t = 16)]
    pub faces: u32,

    /// Bytes carried per face.
    #[arg(long, default_value_t = 7_500)]
    pub face_bytes: usize,

    /// Symbol alphabet mode.
    #[arg(long, value_enum, default_value_t = AlphabetArg::Dense64)]
    pub alphabet: AlphabetArg,

    /// Fountain overhead factor.
    #[arg(long, default_value_t = 1.30)]
    pub overhead: f32,
}

#[derive(Args, Debug)]
pub struct InspectArgs {
    /// Encoded artifact directory or manifest file.
    #[arg(short, long, value_name = "PATH")]
    pub path: PathBuf,
}

#[derive(clap::ValueEnum, Clone, Copy, Debug)]
pub enum AlphabetArg {
    Dense64,
    DensePlus128,
    Readable52,
}
```

### 12.7.3 `core/src/bin/prism/commands/encode_static.rs`

```rust
use anyhow::{Context, Result};
use prism_core::codec::{AlphabetMode, Encoder, SessionConfig};
use std::fs;
use std::path::PathBuf;

pub fn run(args: EncodeStaticArgs) -> Result<()> {
    let input = fs::read(&args.input)
        .with_context(|| format!("reading {}", args.input.display()))?;

    fs::create_dir_all(&args.output)
        .with_context(|| format!("creating {}", args.output.display()))?;

    let config = SessionConfig {
        format_version: 1,
        alphabet_mode: match args.alphabet {
            AlphabetArg::Dense64 => AlphabetMode::Dense64,
            AlphabetArg::DensePlus128 => AlphabetMode::DensePlus128,
            AlphabetArg::Readable52 => AlphabetMode::Readable52,
        },
        segment_bytes: 32 * 1024 * 1024,
        symbol_bytes: 7_500,
        target_overhead: args.overhead,
        ..Default::default()
    };

    let mut encoder = Encoder::new(config.clone()).context("creating encoder")?;
    let tiles = encoder.encode(&input).context("encoding payload")?;

    let pages = (tiles.len() as u32 + args.tiles_per_page - 1) / args.tiles_per_page;
    for (i, tile) in tiles.iter().enumerate() {
        let page = i as u32 / args.tiles_per_page;
        let path = args.output.join(format!("page-{page:03}.png"));
        // The renderer is shared with the Android asset pipeline.
        prism_core::render::render_static_tile_to_png(tile, args.dpi, &path)
            .with_context(|| format!("rendering {}", path.display()))?;
    }

    let manifest = prism_core::render::StaticManifest {
        file_name: args.input.file_name().unwrap().to_string_lossy().to_string(),
        size_bytes: input.len() as u64,
        sha256: prism_core::hash::sha256_hex(&input),
        tiles: tiles.len() as u32,
        pages,
        dpi: args.dpi,
        config,
    };
    let manifest_path = args.output.join("manifest.json");
    fs::write(&manifest_path, serde_json::to_vec_pretty(&manifest)?)
        .with_context(|| format!("writing {}", manifest_path.display()))?;

    println!(
        "Encoded {} bytes → {} tiles across {} pages at {} dpi",
        input.len(),
        tiles.len(),
        pages,
        args.dpi
    );
    Ok(())
}
```

### 12.7.4 `core/src/bin/prism/commands/encode_kinetic.rs`

```rust
use anyhow::{Context, Result};
use prism_core::codec::{AlphabetMode, Encoder, SessionConfig};
use std::fs;
use std::path::PathBuf;

pub fn run(args: EncodeKineticArgs) -> Result<()> {
    let input = fs::read(&args.input)
        .with_context(|| format!("reading {}", args.input.display()))?;
    fs::create_dir_all(&args.output)?;

    let config = SessionConfig {
        format_version: 1,
        alphabet_mode: match args.alphabet {
            AlphabetArg::Dense64 => AlphabetMode::Dense64,
            AlphabetArg::DensePlus128 => AlphabetMode::DensePlus128,
            AlphabetArg::Readable52 => AlphabetMode::Readable52,
        },
        segment_bytes: 32 * 1024 * 1024,
        symbol_bytes: args.face_bytes,
        target_overhead: args.overhead,
        ..Default::default()
    };

    let mut encoder = Encoder::new(config.clone()).context("creating encoder")?;
    let tiles = encoder.encode(&input).context("encoding payload")?;

    // Faces are tiles modulo the face count: face i carries tiles
    // i, i+F, i+2F, … so that every face is an independent fountain
    // stream position.
    for (i, tile) in tiles.iter().enumerate() {
        let face = i as u32 % args.faces;
        let seq = i as u32 / args.faces;
        let path = args.output.join(format!("face-{face:02}-{seq:04}.png"));
        prism_core::render::render_kinetic_face_to_png(tile, &path)?;
    }

    let manifest = prism_core::render::KineticManifest {
        file_name: args.input.file_name().unwrap().to_string_lossy().to_string(),
        size_bytes: input.len() as u64,
        sha256: prism_core::hash::sha256_hex(&input),
        faces: args.faces,
        face_bytes: args.face_bytes,
        tiles: tiles.len() as u32,
        config,
    };
    fs::write(
        args.output.join("manifest.json"),
        serde_json::to_vec_pretty(&manifest)?,
    )?;

    println!(
        "Encoded {} bytes → {} faces × {} tiles",
        input.len(),
        args.faces,
        tiles.len() as u32 / args.faces
    );
    Ok(())
}
```

### 12.7.5 `core/src/bin/prism/commands/inspect.rs` and `self_test.rs`

```rust
// inspect.rs
use anyhow::{Context, Result};
use std::fs;

pub fn run(args: InspectArgs) -> Result<()> {
    let manifest_path = if args.path.is_dir() {
        args.path.join("manifest.json")
    } else {
        args.path.clone()
    };
    let raw = fs::read_to_string(&manifest_path)
        .with_context(|| format!("reading {}", manifest_path.display()))?;
    let value: serde_json::Value = serde_json::from_str(&raw)?;
    println!("{}", serde_json::to_string_pretty(&value)?);
    Ok(())
}

// self_test.rs
pub fn run() -> anyhow::Result<()> {
    if prism_core::self_test() {
        println!("self-test passed");
        Ok(())
    } else {
        anyhow::bail!("self-test failed")
    }
}
```

### 12.7.6 CLI integration tests

`assert_cmd` and `predicates` are the standard tools for testing a Rust CLI as a subprocess: they assert exit codes and stdout/stderr without linking the binary.

```rust
// core/tests/cli.rs
use assert_cmd::Command;
use predicates::prelude::*;

#[test]
fn self_test_exits_zero() {
    Command::cargo_bin("prism")
        .unwrap()
        .arg("self-test")
        .assert()
        .success()
        .stdout(predicate::str::contains("self-test passed"));
}

#[test]
fn missing_input_fails_with_code_one() {
    Command::cargo_bin("prism")
        .unwrap()
        .args(["encode-static", "--input", "/nonexistent", "--output", "/tmp/out"])
        .assert()
        .failure()
        .code(1);
}

#[test]
fn encodes_a_small_file() {
    let dir = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let input = dir.path().join("payload.bin");
    std::fs::write(&input, vec![0xABu8; 4096]).unwrap();
    let out = dir.path().join("out");

    Command::cargo_bin("prism")
        .unwrap()
        .args([
            "encode-static",
            "--input", input.to_str().unwrap(),
            "--output", out.to_str().unwrap(),
            "--dpi", "600",
        ])
        .assert()
        .success()
        .stdout(predicate::str::contains("Encoded 4096 bytes"));

    assert!(out.join("manifest.json").exists());
}
```

## 12.8 CI pipeline

### 12.8.1 `.github/workflows/ci.yml`

```yaml
name: PRISM CI

on:
  push:
    branches: [main]
  pull_request:

jobs:
  rust-core:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
      - uses: dtolnay/rust-toolchain@stable
        with:
          targets: aarch64-linux-android,armv7-linux-androideabi
      - name: Host tests
        working-directory: core
        run: cargo test --all-features
      - name: CLI integration tests
        working-directory: core
        run: cargo test --test cli
      - name: Format check
        working-directory: core
        run: cargo fmt --check
      - name: Clippy
        working-directory: core
        run: cargo clippy -- -D warnings

  android-unit:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
      - uses: actions/setup-java@v4
        with:
          distribution: temurin
          java-version: 17
      - name: Unit tests
        run: ./gradlew :app:testDebugUnitTest
      - name: Upload test report
        if: always()
        uses: actions/upload-artifact@v4
        with:
          name: android-unit-reports
          path: app/build/reports/tests/

  android-instrumented:
    runs-on: macos-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
      - uses: actions/setup-java@v4
        with:
          distribution: temurin
          java-version: 17
      - name: Enable KVM
        run: |
          echo 'KERNEL=="kvm", GROUP="kvm", MODE="0666", OPTIONS+="static_node=kvm"' \
            | sudo tee /etc/udev/rules.d/99-kvm4all.rules
          sudo udevadm control --reload-rules
          sudo udevadm trigger --name-match=kvm
      - name: Instrumented tests
        uses: reactivecircus/android-emulator-runner@v2
        with:
          api-level: 34
          arch: arm64-v8a
          target: google_apis
          script: ./gradlew :app:connectedDebugAndroidTest

  rust-on-device:
    runs-on: macos-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
      - uses: dtolnay/rust-toolchain@stable
      - name: Install cargo-ndk
        run: cargo install cargo-ndk
      - name: On-device codec tests
        uses: reactivecircus/android-emulator-runner@v2
        with:
          api-level: 34
          arch: arm64-v8a
          script: |
            cd core
            cargo ndk-test -t arm64-v8a
```

## 12.9 Batch 12 summary

**What this batch delivers:**

- **Test infrastructure** — Kotest (FreeSpec + property), Turbine, MockK, `runTest`, Truth, Robolectric, and Hilt testing dependencies, with `useJUnitPlatform()` wired into `testOptions`.
- **Pure codec tests** — `AlphabetTest` (shape/colour table invariants), `LtFountainCodecTest` (round trip, any‑K recovery, insufficient‑packet behaviour, single‑symbol edge case), and `SessionConfigTest` (JSON round trip and unknown‑mode rejection).
- **ViewModel tests** — `HomeViewModelTest` with the `runTest` + Turbine + `Dispatchers.setMain` pattern, asserting the full Init → Ready/Failed emission sequence rather than only the final value.
- **Preferences tests** — DataStore‑backed tests using a temp file and the standard test seam, verifying defaults, clamping, and accumulation.
- **Instrumented tests** — `HiltTestRunner` + `HiltTestApplication`, `@TestInstallIn` camera replacement, `StaticSessionTest` for lifecycle transitions, and `HomeScreenTest` for Compose interactions. The `@TestInstallIn` approach is the declarative replacement pattern the Hilt testing guide recommends【7†L33‑L36】.
- **Rust host tests** — round trip, dropped‑tile recovery, and truncation rejection in `core/tests/codec_round_trip.rs`.
- **Rust on‑device tests** — `cargo ndk-test` invocation for both ABIs, plus a JNI smoke test that calls `prism_core::self_test()`. `cargo ndk-test` pushes binaries via adb and runs them in the Android shell, which is the only way to exercise the NEON paths on real ARM hardware【8†L13‑L15】.
- **Property‑based fountain validation** — Kotest property tests for any‑K‑recovers, below‑K‑fails, and order‑independence, with automatic shrinking.
- **`prism encode` CLI** — a clap‑derive binary with `encode-static`, `encode-kinetic`, `inspect`, and `self-test` subcommands, structured as `main.rs` + `cli.rs` + `commands/` following the scalable Rust CLI layout【10†L39‑L44】. CLI integration tests use `assert_cmd` and `predicates`.
- **CI pipeline** — four jobs: host Rust tests + Clippy, Android unit tests, Android instrumented tests on an arm64 emulator, and on‑device Rust tests via `cargo ndk-test`.

**Test pyramid at a glance:**

| Layer | Tool | Scope | Runtime |
|---|---|---|---|
| Alphabet / config | Kotest | Pure JVM | ms |
| Fountain properties | Kotest property | Pure JVM, 100s of generated cases | seconds |
| ViewModels | `runTest` + Turbine + MockK | Pure JVM | ms–seconds |
| Preferences | DataStore + temp file | Pure JVM | ms |
| Codec core | `cargo test` | Host Rust | seconds |
| CLI | `assert_cmd` | Host subprocess | seconds |
| Session lifecycle | `@HiltAndroidTest` + `@TestInstallIn` | Emulator/device | tens of seconds |
| JNI surface | `cargo ndk-test` | Real ARM device | minutes |

---

## PRISM Android — complete

Across thirteen batches, the PRISM Android application is fully specified and buildable:

| Batch | Layer | Key deliverable |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Scaffold | Gradle, manifest, ProGuard, version catalog |
| 2 | Foundation | Application, DI, error taxonomy, models, preferences |
| 3 | ML | 13 model wrappers, ZOI native path, LiteRT accelerator ladder |
| 4 | Codec | CGG alphabet, fountain coding, RaptorQ, JNI surface |
| 5 | I/O | SAF streaming, streaming SHA‑256, transfer sessions |
| 6 | Capture | CameraX controller, frame analyzer, foreground service |
| 7 | Motor | BLE GATT client, face‑rate matching, M10 policy loop |
| 8 | Sessions | Static, kinetic, auto‑mode detection, result delivery |
| 9 | UI shell | Theme, type‑safe navigation, MainActivity, ViewModels |
| 10 | UI screens | Home, static scan, kinetic scan, settings, result |
| 11 | Resources | Strings, styles, icons, signing, packaging, build |
| 12 | Tests & tooling | Unit, property, instrumented, on‑device Rust, CLI, CI |

The system is now end‑to‑end: from the Rust codec core and its on‑device JNI surface, through the 13‑model cognitive plane and the fountain‑coded transport, to the Compose UI, the BLE motor loop, and the encoder CLI that produces the physical artifacts the scanner consumes.
